{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_02_NISFAoIOaWkyowOTvFkh5421","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I now have a comprehensive understanding. Let me compile the findings into a detailed report.\n\n## Detailed Findings: TetrisAttack Reward Function and RLVE Reward Processing\n\n### 1. How the reward function works\n\n**Core file:** `/workspace/Gym/environments/tetris_attack/environment.py`\n\nThe environment's `scorer()` (lines 269-314) is the reward function. It returns a float in `[-1.0, +1.0]`.\n\n**The mechanism:**\n1. **Parse** the model output into a list of swap indices via `_process()` (line 256): splits the text inside the answer markers on whitespace and casts each token to `int`. Returns `None` on malformed input.\n2. **Simulate** the swaps with `simulate_swaps()` (line 64): first reduces the initial array (stack-based removal of adjacent equal elements via `reduce()`), then applies each swap followed by reduction. The simulation **stops early** on the first out-of-bounds index or once the array becomes empty. It returns `(final_array, valid_swaps)`.\n3. **Score:**\n   - If parsing fails and the optimal answer is 0 (array already empty/trivial): reward = `1.0`\n   - If parsing fails otherwise, or the final array is **not empty** (not all elements cleared): reward = `-1.0`\n   - If cleared with `S` swaps and the optimal is `S*`:\n     - `S <= S*` → reward = `1.0`\n     - `S > S*` → `score = 1.0 - 2.0*(S - S*) / max(S*, 1)`, clamped to `[-1.0, 1.0]`\n\nSo the reward is a **linear partial-credit schedule**: it degrades from `1.0` at the optimal swap count to `-1.0` at `2×` the optimal count.\n\n**Meaning of specific values:**\n- **`1.0`**: The answer cleared the entire array using at most the optimal number of swaps. This is the \"fully correct\" reward.\n- **`-0.5`**: The answer cleared the entire array but used **more swaps than optimal** — specifically, `S ≈ 1.75 × S*`. Example: `S* = 4, S = 7` → `1.0 - 2*(7-4)/4 = -0.5`. It is a valid-but-suboptimal solution receiving partial negative credit. (Because `S` and `S*` are integers, `-0.5` is reached exactly only for `S*` divisible by 4; other suboptimal counts give nearby values like `-0.333`.)\n- **`-1.0`**: Either the array was **not cleared**, the parse failed (with a non-trivial optimum), an out-of-bounds swap terminated the sequence early before clearing, or `S >= 2 × S*`.\n\nI verified the formula empirically:\n\n```\nS_star=4: S=5→0.5, S=6→0.0, S=7→-0.5, S=8→-1.0\nS_star=2: S=3→0.0, S=4→-1.0\nS_star=3: S=4→0.333, S=5→-0.333, S=6→-1.0\n```\n\n### 2. `format_score` and `accuracy` components\n\n**Defined in the base class:** `/workspace/Gym/environment.py`, `verifier()` (lines 179-194):\n\n```python\neps = 1E-6\nreturn dict(\n    reward = score,                        # [-1.0, +1.0] continuous score\n    accuracy = int(score >= self.passing_reward_threshold - eps),  # 0 or 1\n    format_score = int(score >= -1.0 + eps),                        # 0 or 1\n)\n```\n\n- **`reward`** — the raw continuous score from the environment (`scorer()`).\n- **`accuracy`** — binary \"fully correct\": `1` iff `score >= 1.0` (since `passing_reward_threshold = 1.0` by default). For TetrisAttack this means exactly the optimal-or-better clearing sequence.\n- **`format_score`** — binary, `1` iff `score > -1.0`. **Important nuance:** this is *not* a genuine format/parse-quality check. For TetrisAttack, `format_score = 1` for any answer that clears with fewer than `2×S*` swaps (even a suboptimal `-0.5` answer), and `0` only when the hard `-1.0` penalty is applied (array not cleared, parse failure, or `>= 2×S*` swaps). Contrast with `bbeh.py` (`/workspace/slime/rollout/rm_hub/bbeh.py:126-142`), where `format_score` genuinely reflects whether the `\\boxed{}` format was present.\n\n**How these flow through training:**\n- `rlve_rm()` (`/workspace/slime/rollout/rm_hub/rlve_rm.py:17-19`) returns this dict verbatim as `sample.reward`.\n- `Sample.get_reward_value()` (`/workspace/slime/utils/types.py:46-47`) selects a single key: `sample.reward[args.reward_key]`.\n- Training scripts set `--reward-key reward`, so the **continuous score** is used as the RL reward. The README (`/workspace/README.md:159-164`) documents that you can switch to binary rewards with `--reward-key accuracy`.\n- During evaluation, `/workspace/evaluation/evaluate.py:213-214` reads `config[\"accuracy_key\"]` (set to `\"accuracy\"` in `data/HELD-OUT_ENVIRONMENTS/evaluation_config.json`).\n\n### 3. Environment-specific reward logic for TetrisAttack\n\n**Optimal answer computation** (in `_generate`, lines 200-243):\n- `N <= 5`: exact **BFS** (`bfs_optimal`) → true minimum swaps.\n- `N == 6`: BFS limited to 200,000 states; falls back to greedy if not found.\n- Larger `N` (or N=6 BFS failure): **multi-start randomized greedy** (`greedy_solve`, 50 restarts, lines 180-194).\n\n**Potential reward-gaming / robustness concerns:**\n\n1. **Greedy fallback overestimates the optimum.** For N > 5 (or N=6 when BFS times out), `optimal_swaps` is the best greedy solution found, which is always `>=` the true minimum. If `S*` is inflated, a suboptimal model answer with `S > true_optimum` but `S <= S*` still gets `reward = 1.0` and `accuracy = 1`. This makes scoring lenient for harder instances.\n\n2. **`format_score` is near-meaningless for this environment** (as noted above) — it does not distinguish formatting failures, only the hard `-1.0` penalty.\n\n3. **Swap padding after clearing is harmless, but in-bounds padding before clearing counts.** `simulate_swaps` stops incrementing `valid_swaps` once the array empties (line 78-79), so trailing garbage after clearing doesn't hurt. But extra *in-bounds* swaps before the array clears do count toward `S`, which is the intended suboptimality penalty.\n\n4. **No cap on the number of parsed swaps.** `_process` accepts any length list. A very long answer is fully parsed/simulated (bounded by the early-break conditions), which is deterministic but could be a minor DoS vector if the output is huge.\n\n5. **Data/code version mismatch risk.** The pre-built eval data in `/workspace/data/SCAN_GRPO/prompts_8.json` stores TetrisAttack metadata configs with parameter keys `A`, `gold_answer`, `reference_answer` (and an older prompt format `A[0]=2 A[1]=0 ...`). The **current** environment code (`_generate`, lines 216-243) stores `initial_array` and `optimal_swaps`, and the prompt uses a Python-list format. Since `scorer` looks up `initial_array` / `optimal_swaps` (lines 284-290), scoring pre-built samples built with the old keys would return `-1.0` for every sample (both `parameter.get(...)` return `None`). (Because `S` and `S*` are integers, `-0.5` is reached exactly only for `S*` divisible by 4; other suboptimal counts give nearby values like `-0.333`.)\n- **`-1.0`**: Either the array was **not cleared**, the parse failed (with a non-trivial optimum), an out-of-bounds swap terminated the sequence early before clearing, or `S >= 2 × S*`.\n\nI verified the formula empirically:\n\n```\nS_star=4: S=5→0.5, S=6→0.0, S=7→-0.5, S=8→-1.0\nS_star=2: S=3→0.0, S=4→-1.0\nS_star=3: S=4→0.333, S=5→-0.333, S=6→-1.0\n```\n\n### 2. `format_score` and `accuracy` components\n\n**Defined in the base class:** `/workspace/Gym/environment.py`, `verifier()` (lines 179-194):\n\n```python\neps = 1E-6\nreturn dict(\n    reward = score,                        # [-1.0, +1.0] continuous score\n    accuracy = int(score >= self.passing_reward_threshold - eps),  # 0 or 1\n    format_score = int(score >= -1.0 + eps),                        # 0 or 1\n)\n```\n\n- **`reward`** — the raw continuous score from the environment (`scorer()`).\n- **`accuracy`** — binary \"fully correct\": `1` iff `score >= 1.0` (since `passing_reward_threshold = 1.0` by default). For TetrisAttack this means exactly the optimal-or-better clearing sequence.\n- **`format_score`** — binary, `1` iff `score > -1.0`. **Important nuance:** this is *not* a genuine format/parse-quality check. For TetrisAttack, `format_score = 1` for any answer that clears with fewer than `2×S*` swaps (even a suboptimal `-0.5` answer), and `0` only when the hard `-1.0` penalty is applied (array not cleared, parse failure, or `>= 2×S*` swaps). Contrast with `bbeh.py` (`/workspace/slime/rollout/rm_hub/bbeh.py:126-142`), where `format_score` genuinely reflects whether the `\\boxed{}` format was present.\n\n**How these flow through training:**\n- `rlve_rm()` (`/workspace/slime/rollout/rm_hub/rlve_rm.py:17-19`) returns this dict verbatim as `sample.reward`.\n- `Sample.get_reward_value()` (`/workspace/slime/utils/types.py:46-47`) selects a single key: `sample.reward[args.reward_key]`.\n- Training scripts set `--reward-key reward`, so the **continuous score** is used as the RL reward. The README (`/workspace/README.md:159-164`) documents that you can switch to binary rewards with `--reward-key accuracy`.\n- During evaluation, `/workspace/evaluation/evaluate.py:213-214` reads `config[\"accuracy_key\"]` (set to `\"accuracy\"` in `data/HELD-OUT_ENVIRONMENTS/evaluation_config.json`).\n\n### 3. Environment-specific reward logic for TetrisAttack\n\n**Optimal answer computation** (in `_generate`, lines 200-243):\n- `N <= 5`: exact **BFS** (`bfs_optimal`) → true minimum swaps.\n- `N == 6`: BFS limited to 200,000 states; falls back to greedy if not found.\n- Larger `N` (or N=6 BFS failure): **multi-start randomized greedy** (`greedy_solve`, 50 restarts, lines 180-194).\n\n**Potential reward-gaming / robustness concerns:**\n\n1. **Greedy fallback overestimates the optimum.** For N > 5 (or N=6 when BFS times out), `optimal_swaps` is the best greedy solution found, which is always `>=` the true minimum. If `S*` is inflated, a suboptimal model answer with `S > true_optimum` but `S <= S*` still gets `reward = 1.0` and `accuracy = 1`. This makes scoring lenient for harder instances.\n\n2. **`format_score` is near-meaningless for this environment** (as noted above) — it does not distinguish formatting failures, only the hard `-1.0` penalty.\n\n3. **Swap padding after clearing is harmless, but in-bounds padding before clearing counts.** `simulate_swaps` stops incrementing `valid_swaps` once the array empties (line 78-79), so trailing garbage after clearing doesn't hurt. But extra *in-bounds* swaps before the array clears do count toward `S`, which is the intended suboptimality penalty.\n\n4. **No cap on the number of parsed swaps.** `_process` accepts any length list. A very long answer is fully parsed/simulated (bounded by the early-break conditions), which is deterministic but could be a minor DoS vector if the output is huge.\n\n5. **Data/code version mismatch risk.** The pre-built eval data in `/workspace/data/SCAN_GRPO/prompts_8.json` stores TetrisAttack metadata configs with parameter keys `A`, `gold_answer`, `reference_answer` (and an older prompt format `A[0]=2 A[1]=0 ...`). The **current** environment code (`_generate`, lines 216-243) stores `initial_array` and `optimal_swaps`, and the prompt uses a Python-list format. Since `scorer` looks up `initial_array` / `optimal_swaps` (lines 284-290), scoring pre-built samples built with the old keys would return `-1.0` for every sample (both `parameter.get(...)` return `None`). The environment file was modified later (Jul 15 18:46) than the data (Jul 6 08:09), so this is a stale-data concern.\n\n### Reward processing in `/workspace/slime` and `/workspace/slime_plugins`\n\n**slime (RLVE reward handling):**\n- `/workspace/slime/rollout/rm_hub/rlve_rm.py` — The **RLVE reward type handler**.
